# Proposed OEIS comments: A275402 to A281561

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A275402

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A275402>  
Result obtained: **3 September 2026**  
Paper: [3867](../papers/03501-04000/03867-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 4*a(n-1) + 6*a(n-2) + 4*a(n-3).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(3 + 4*x + 2*x^2) / (1 - 4*x - 6*x^2 - 4*x^3). - _Colin Barker_, Feb 04 2019

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 25 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 3. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A277560

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A277560>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [4269](../papers/04001-04500/04269-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Nov 06 2016: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 7 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

## A277653

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A277653>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [10298](../papers/10001-10500/10298-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 3*a(n-1) + 2*a(n-2) - 2*a(n-4).
> Empirical g.f.: x^3*(1 - x) / (1 - 3*x - 2*x^2 + 2*x^4). - _Colin Barker_, Feb 05 2019

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Every cell the condition names lies a bounded number of lines away -- two of them, where it reaches a knight's move -- so a window of that width decides the condition on the line in the middle, and the lines with nothing far enough beyond them are decided at the end. Counting a line at a time therefore gives a transfer matrix with 6 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A277769

Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [10884](../papers/10501-11000/10884-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 98 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 134 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 98, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Every cell the condition names lies a bounded number of lines away -- two of them, where it reaches a knight's move -- so a window of that width decides the condition on the line in the middle, and the lines with nothing far enough beyond them are decided at the end. Counting a line at a time therefore gives a transfer matrix with 5 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 3. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A277797

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A277797>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [4227](../papers/04001-04500/04227-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Nov 01 2016: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 8 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A278017

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A278017>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [11249](../papers/11001-11500/11249-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 63 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 114 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 63, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A278023

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A278023>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [11471](../papers/11001-11500/11471-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: +n*(3*n^2-12*n+11) *a(n) -(3*n-5) *(3*n^2-9*n+4) *a(n-1) -2*(2*n-5) *(3*n^2-6*n+2) *a(n-2) +n *(3*n^2-12*n+11) *a(n-3) -2 *(2*n-5) *(3*n^2-6*n+2) *a(n-4)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jun 24 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 4, so the recurrence holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A278472

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A278472>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [11447](../papers/11001-11500/11447-PROOF.pdf), [11617](../papers/11501-12000/11617-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: +(n+2)*(n^2-4*n+1)*a(n) +2*(-4*n^3+9*n^2+16*n-6)*a(n-1) +(15*n^3-38*n^2-9*n+14)*a(n-2) +2*(2*n-1)*(n^2-2*n-2)*a(n-3)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Mar 06 2017
> Conjecture: +(n+2)*a(n) +(-11*n-14)*a(n-1) +(37*n+20)*a(n-2) +(-25*n-6)*a(n-3) +2*(-21*n+41)*a(n-4) +4*(-2*n+5)*a(n-5)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Mar 06 2017

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 2, so the recurrence holds for n > 2. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A278665

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A278665>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [4134](../papers/04001-04500/04134-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Nov 26 2016: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 19 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

> Conjecture: +n*(3*n-62)*a(n) +(3*n^2+362*n-247)*a(n-1) +(-171*n^2+220*n+162)*a(n-2) +(417*n^2-2570*n+3551)*a(n-3) +2*(27*n-59)*(2*n-7)*a(n-4)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Mar 12 2017
> Conjecture: +n*(n^2-11*n+22)*a(n) +2*(-4*n^3+45*n^2-113*n+60)*a(n-1) +(15*n^3-173*n^2+530*n-480)*a(n-2) +2*(2*n-5)*(n^2-9*n+12)*a(n-3)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Mar 12 2017

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 3, so the recurrence holds for n > 3. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A279028

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A279028>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [4294](../papers/04001-04500/04294-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Dec 04 2016: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 6 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

```

## A279498

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A279498>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [4138](../papers/04001-04500/04138-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Dec 14 2016: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 17 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A279872

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A279872>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [4298](../papers/04001-04500/04298-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures from _Chai Wah Wu_, Aug 02 2021: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 6 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A279959

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A279959>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [4202](../papers/04001-04500/04202-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Dec 24 2016: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 9 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A280367

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A280367>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [4238](../papers/04001-04500/04238-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Jan 01 2017: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 8 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A280410

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A280410>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [4299](../papers/04001-04500/04299-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Jan 03 2017: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 6 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [1007](../papers/01001-01500/01007-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 5*a(n-1) - 7*a(n-2) - 2*a(n-3) + 10*a(n-4) - 2*a(n-5) - 5*a(n-6) + a(n-7) + a(n-8) for n>15.
> Empirical g.f.: x^2*(6 + 38*x - 59*x^2 - 89*x^3 + 62*x^4 - 51*x^5 + 175*x^6 + 166*x^7 - 217*x^8 - 106*x^9 + 71*x^10 + 21*x^11 - 7*x^12 - x^13) / ((1 - x)^2*(1 - x - x^2)^3). - _Colin Barker_, Feb 15 2019

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 195 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 15. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A281146

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A281146>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [4242](../papers/04001-04500/04242-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Feb 22 2017: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 8 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A281214

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A281214>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [4167](../papers/04001-04500/04167-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Jan 18 2017: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 12 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A281473

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A281473>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [2993](../papers/02501-03000/02993-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) - a(n-2) + a(n-3) - a(n-4) + a(n-5) -a (n-6) for n>11.
> Empirical g.f.: x*(16 + 232*x - 379*x^2 + 189*x^3 - 251*x^4 + 124*x^5 - 288*x^6 + 132*x^7 - 24*x^8 + x^9 + 2*x^10) / ((1 - x)*(1 - x - x^3 - x^5)). - _Colin Barker_, Feb 19 2019

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 561 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```
